The reporting gap is the finding to carry forward: **S2 has no GitHub advisory**, so `repos/<o>/<r>/security-advisories` cannot be treated as the security history of this action, and any future re-verification must include a search of independent security research, not only that endpoint. That is written into the `re-verify:` line.

No `unsafe` exists (no Rust code). (4) **`ubuntu-latest, macos-latest`** — inapplicable *as a matrix gate*, with the fact stated: each member is a single non-matrix Linux job in `py`, in both examples and in both maintainer workflows; adding a macOS runner would double the spend for no additional signal, because the reviewed artifact is a diff, not a build. Windows, noted not required: partial, and the figure is exact rather than asserted — `action.yml` at `v1.0.220` carries **three** `runner.os` guards, two `!= 'Windows'` and one `== 'Linux'`, and all three are additionally gated on `inputs.allowed_non_write_users != ''`, which this decision leaves at its default `""`, so **none of the three is reached by the recommended configuration** (`action-source-v1.0.220.log`, revision-2 addendum, HTTP 200 / exit 0). (5) **Default features / async-runtime coupling** — inapplicable (no Cargo features, no Rust runtime). The action defaults that *do* matter, read from `action.yml` at `v1.0.220`: `allowed_bots: ""` ("Empty string (default) allows no bots"), `allowed_non_write_users: ""`, `trigger_phrase: "@claude"`, `label_trigger: "claude"`, `assignee_trigger` unset, `branch_prefix: "claude/"`, `use_commit_signing: "false"`, `bot_name: "claude[bot]"`, `additional_permissions: ""`. Note `label_trigger` defaults to `"claude"` — but that default is **inert under the recommended `on:` blocks**, which list no `issues: [labeled]` type, so applying a `claude` label starts no workflow run at all. It becomes a trigger path a repository did not configure only if a later revision adds `labeled` to the assistant's event list, which is the reason to record it here. (6) **Binary size / compile time** — inapplicable; the analogue: no `cargo` invocation, therefore zero added compile time; fixed overhead is a checkout plus a Bun install; variable cost is one agent session per triggering event, unmeasurable per `#1482`.

**Baseline findings.** Every `BASELINE-REVIEW:` line any engine raised is carried here in the required `BASELINE-REVIEW: F### — principle — proposed change — evidence` form (`research/RUNBOOK.md` §P02-T01). Revision 2 carried two and stated that `opus` was the only engine to raise any; both statements were false, and revision 3 withdraws them. A controller check with an anchored `grep -c '^BASELINE-REVIEW:'` finds **six lines across four of the five reports** (`codex` 1, `opus` 2, `doxa-openai` 2, `doxa-perplexity` 1, `doxa-gemini` 0); `doxa-gemini` raised a **seventh** at `raw/doxa-gemini.normalized.md:25` wrapped in backticks, which an anchored grep cannot see. **Seven were raised in substance; all seven are carried below.** `research/RUNBOOK.md` §4 makes the controller — not this item — the adjudicator of these findings "with the affected decision owners", reconciling ledger, index, prompt and dependency changes together. R18's position on each is recorded so the controller has it; **routing is not conditional on R18 agreeing**, and the two R18 rejects are routed exactly like the two it adopts.

BASELINE-REVIEW: F051 — a least-privilege grant must never silently disable the workflow's only output — raise the review job's `pull-requests: read` to `pull-requests: write` — evidence: `py .github/workflows/claude-code-review.yml` at pinned commit `b08bccfb` grants `contents: read` / `pull-requests: read` / `issues: read` / `id-token: write` while instructing the `code-review` plugin to post with `--comment` (read verbatim, `source-repos-pinned.log`, exit 0), whereas all three maintainer references grant `pull-requests: write` — `anthropics/claude-code-action` `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml` and `examples/pr-review-comprehensive.yml` at `v1.0.220`, and `docs/setup.md`'s federation sample (`action-source-v1.0.220.log`, HTTP 200) — and open issue `anthropics/claude-code-action#1121` ("claude-code-review workflow template generates insufficient permissions; action silently swallows write failure", opened 2026-03-24, still `open` at 2026-09-10) states "Posting review comments requires `pull-requests: write`" and reports the `read` variant producing a green job that posts nothing, independently reproduced in-thread on 2026-05-09 by an account whose `author_association` is `NONE`, i.e. an unrelated third party (`action-issues.log`; <https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code-action/issues/1121>, <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code-action/v1.0.220/.github/workflows/claude-review.yml>, <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code-action/v1.0.220/examples/pr-review-comprehensive.yml>, all retrieved 2026-09-10 UTC). Raised by `opus` (`raw/opus.md:93`). Affected items: **R18** (this item) and **R08**, which owns the repository-wide permission-hardening convention this row must not be read as overriding. **R18's position: adopted**, with limits below.

BASELINE-REVIEW: F051 and F052 — minimize long-lived credentials in a public template — prefer workload identity federation over a static `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` repository secret — evidence: both `py` files at `b08bccfb` pass `claude_code_oauth_token: ${{ secrets.CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN }}` (`source-repos-pinned.log`); `docs/setup.md#workload-identity-federation` at `v1.0.220` documents exchanging the workflow's GitHub OIDC token for a short-lived Anthropic access token — "no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` secret to create, store, or rotate" — via `anthropic_federation_rule_id`, `anthropic_organization_id` and `anthropic_service_account_id`, values the document calls "identifiers, not credentials, so they can live directly in the workflow file (or in repository variables)", and **both** maintainer workflows at `v1.0.220` use exactly this with no `secrets.` reference anywhere (`action-source-v1.0.220.log`). Federation requires `id-token: write`, which `docs/setup.md` notes "the default GitHub App authentication path already requires", so it costs no additional permission (<https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code-action/v1.0.220/docs/setup.md>, <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code-action/v1.0.220/.github/workflows/claude.yml>, retrieved 2026-09-10 UTC; the vendor's own federation reference is <https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/manage-claude/workload-identity-federation>). Raised by `opus` (`raw/opus.md:95`). Affected items: **R18** and **R21**, which owns bot-credential sourcing for the contributors bot and should not be read as constrained by this row. **R18's position: adopted**, with limits below, and with the correction that federation is **not** a mitigation for `#1068`.

BASELINE-REVIEW: F051/F052 — optional AI assistance must not impose a paid credential, privileged GitHub App, or provider run on every public-template consumer — change the baseline from enabled `py`-derived workflows to an absent-by-default, documented opt-in pair — evidence: Anthropic requires provider authentication and repository authority, while GitHub withholds ordinary secrets from fork PRs; retrieved 2026-09-06: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code-action/main/docs/setup.md>; <https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/events-that-trigger-workflows>. Raised by `codex` (`raw/codex.md:21`). Affected items: **R18**. **R18's position: not adopted as the baseline change, and the reason is narrow, restated at revision 6 against the mechanism that replaced the flag** — credential presence makes the pair absent *in effect* for every consumer (no credential ⇒ no credentialed run, by design; the skipped-not-failed outcome is acceptance criterion **B4**), which the empirical checks show is expressible through a preflight job whose output a job-level `if:` may read, so the imposition the finding objects to is answered without removing the files — and revision 6 adds the answer the finding's own framing asks for, which is that an adopter who wants the pair truly absent **deletes the two files**, a one-step opt-out that needs no recipe. The finding's underlying judgement — that this should not be on by default anywhere — is the **ranked runner-up**; note that under revision 6 nothing is on by default, because a file with no credential does nothing. Routed unchanged.

BASELINE-REVIEW: F051 — credential-free and non-metered template defaults — do not port the active automatic Claude PR-review workflow; retain it only as an opt-in example and treat removal from `py` as a follow-on project — evidence: automatic runs consume provider capacity, fork and Dependabot events have restricted credentials, and the Rust practice survey found no ecosystem convention requiring it (<https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/events-that-trigger-workflows>). Raised by `doxa-openai` (`raw/doxa-openai.normalized.md:138`). Affected items: **R18**, and **`py-launch-blueprint`** as a follow-on project, which is outside this programme's remit. **R18's position: not adopted**, on the same ground as the `codex` row, and noting the concrete Rust-practice figure that supports it: `org:rust-lang` returns `total_count: 0` for this action, so no Rust convention requires it in either direction. Routed unchanged.

BASELINE-REVIEW: F052 — AI ChatOps is repository-operator policy rather than a language capability — do not port an active `@claude` workflow; document it as a separately adoptable example **and split its ledger decision from F051** — evidence: it has different triggers, write permissions, cost frequency, and actor controls from automatic review (<https://github.com/smorinlabs/py-launch-blueprint/blob/main/.github/workflows/claude.yml>). Raised by `doxa-openai` (`raw/doxa-openai.normalized.md:140`). Affected items: **R18** and **the ledger** (`docs/port/COMMONALITY.md:57-58`, which bundles F051 and F052 to one item). **This row carries a ledger-shaped ask and is routed as such**; R18 cannot split a ledger row and does not attempt to. R18's position on the *design* question is recorded in `## Engines` §6 (bundle, on a stated coupling argument that weakens if `synchronize` is restored); R18's position on the *ledger* question is that it belongs to the controller. Revision 2 discussed the design question and never routed the ledger ask; revision 3 routes it.

BASELINE-REVIEW: F051 — templates should expose a safe, optional capability for AI-assisted PR review and conversational assistance via maintained actions — treat the absence of these workflows in `ts-launch-blueprint` as accidental drift rather than a deliberate policy difference, and align future TypeScript and Rust templates on shipping both workflows with ecosystem-specific trigger tuning — evidence: `anthropics/claude-code-action` supports both automatic review and `@mention` assistance from one action family, and `py`'s implementation shows the two can coexist when guarded against bot-authored PRs and repository permission checks. Raised by `doxa-perplexity` (`raw/doxa-perplexity.normalized.md:41`). Affected items: **R18**, **the ledger** (the `ts` side of F051/F052), and any future `ts` port. **R18's position: rejected with reasons, and routed anyway.** The reason: the finding asserts `ts`'s omission is *accidental*, and the agreement-level argument above reaches the opposite conclusion from the same evidence — the level is policy or mechanism under spec §2, which requires "its owner decision or evidence", and no owner decision registers this pair as a shared value, so an unmatched sibling is what a policy-level agreement without a registered decision looks like, not drift. The finding also offers no evidence *about `ts`* — no `ts` commit, issue or decision is cited — and `TS_PORT_DECISIONS.md` has no entry either way (`inputs/prompt.md`, `## Context`). A claim about another repository's intent needs evidence from that repository. Routing is unaffected: §4 gives the controller the call.

BASELINE-REVIEW: F051, F052 — Policy — adopt bundled AI workflows across templates — the TypeScript template's omission represents accidental drift or a missing capability rather than a justified architectural departure; adopting the bundled workflows in `rs-launch-blueprint` aligns with `py-launch-blueprint` and reinforces baseline AI automation across the organization's foundational templates, with the Python implementation as the definitive reference architecture. Raised by `doxa-gemini` (`raw/doxa-gemini.normalized.md:25`, written inside backticks, which is why an anchored `grep` does not see it). Affected items: **R18**, **the ledger**, and any future `ts` port. **R18's position: rejected with reasons, and routed anyway.** Two reasons, beyond the `doxa-perplexity` row's: the finding's own framing — "the Python implementation serves as the definitive reference architecture" — is the failure mode the research mandate (spec §2, owner amendment A5) exists to prevent, since source implementations are evidence and not predetermined winners; and this decision does *not* take `py` as-is, changing one permission, the credential path, one trigger list, and adding three guards. Routed unchanged.

**Adjudication of the two adopted findings — what the evidence supports and what it does not.** Both are **accepted for the `rs` template**, with limits stated because both are security-relevant.

*F051 / `pull-requests: write`.* **Supported:** that `py`'s grant differs from every maintained reference; that the reference configurations which post comments all grant `write`; that a third party reports and a second party independently reproduces a silent no-op under `read`. **Not supported:** that `py`'s own repository currently exhibits the silent failure — no run of `py`'s workflow was inspected, and `py`'s `--comment` flag differs from the reproducer's configuration, which the reproducer describes as lacking `--comment`. **Not established by this research:** the exact mechanism, i.e. whether the job's `pull-requests` grant bounds the Claude GitHub App installation token used for the write, or whether some writes go through the job's own `GITHUB_TOKEN`. `docs/security.md` says only that "The GitHub app receives only a short-lived token scoped specifically to the repository" and that "the token cannot … perform actions beyond the configured permissions", which does not disambiguate the two. The recommendation therefore rests on *concordance with three maintained references plus a reproduced report*, not on a demonstrated causal chain, and B3 above is the check that settles it in P03. Consequence if the finding is wrong: an over-broad grant on the review job, which R08 would then narrow — a strictly smaller failure than a review that silently never posts.

*F051+F052 / workload identity federation.* **Supported:** that federation exists at `v1.0.220`, that it removes the stored credential, that the maintainer uses it in production, and that it adds no permission beyond the `id-token: write` already required. **Two costs, both verified and both material:** `docs/setup.md` requires **admin access to an Anthropic organization** (register an issuer, create a service account, create a federation rule) — a one-time setup each downstream fork owner must repeat, which a `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` produced by `claude setup-token` does not require; and "Inline comment classification (`classify_inline_comments`) currently requires `anthropic_api_key`; with federation it is skipped and unconfirmed inline comments are posted directly" — a behavior change, not merely a credential change. **Corrected at revision 3:** federation is **not** a mitigation for `#1068`. It removes the static secret from the runner (`#1068`'s second exposure) and leaves the first and third — runner minutes and an obtainable OIDC token — exactly where they were, and the third is what the S2 chain exfiltrates. Revision 2 carried `opus`'s framing of federation as one of two `#1068` mitigations; only clause (d) is one. **Not established:** whether `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` (which `docs/setup.md` describes as generated by Pro and Max users with `claude setup-token`) and a federated service-account token bill against the same account, so the finding is recorded as *prefer federation where the owner has an Anthropic organization and is not relying on subscription-backed OAuth*, and the static token stays documented as the supported fallback. `docs/setup.md` also warns: "Do not set `anthropic_api_key` or `claude_code_oauth_token` alongside the federation inputs — a static credential takes precedence and federation will not be used." **Strengthened at revision 6 in two ways.** First, that precedence warning is now confirmed in source and not only in prose: `setupWorkloadIdentity()` at `base-action/src/workload-identity.ts:128-135` returns `undefined` and emits the matching `core.warning` (check 18). Second, the owner has not said which shape this repository will use, so **revision 6 stops treating federation as the credential path and treats it as one of two supported shapes**, with the preference above intact and unpromoted. The finding's adoption is therefore unchanged in substance — federation is still preferred where the owner has the organization — and the configuration no longer assumes it.

**Minimal realistic example and executable acceptance check.** The minimal example is the two **revision-6** lint fixtures at `review/evidence/candidate-claude-code-review.r5.yml` and `review/evidence/candidate-claude.r5.yml` — the `.r5` suffix is a fixture-generation counter, not a revision number, so these two files, created 2026-09-12, are the fixtures for revision 6 of this decision, plus **one credential in one of the two shapes** — either the secret `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` or `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`, or the variables `ANTHROPIC_FEDERATION_RULE_ID` and `ANTHROPIC_ORGANIZATION_ID` — and the template-local `.claude/commands/review-pr.md` that P03 must author. **No enable variable is part of the example, because revision 6 has none.** `actionlint` on both exits 0 (`actionlint.log`, revision-6 addendum, R6-1); that, the re-run negative control (R6-2) and the relay type-check (R6-4) are the whole of what can be executed before the template exists. Each r5 fixture differs from its r4 predecessor in exactly four respects: the enable-variable clause is removed; a `credential-preflight` job is added that carries the guards, reads both credential shapes from `env`, prints the skip reason and publishes the `credential` output; the real job gains `needs: credential-preflight` and a gate on that output; and the action step gains the two static-credential inputs alongside the three federation identifiers. The assistant fixture additionally gains the bot checks of clause (b). The pinned action version, the `on:` trigger lists, the real jobs' permission blocks and the review lane's `/review-pr` prompt are **unchanged**, which `## Empirical check` check 19 establishes by diff rather than by assertion. The revision-2, revision-3 and revision-4 fixtures are left byte-for-byte as they were linted and audited, and remain in the directory as the prior state. B1–B11 above are the acceptance criteria and every one of them needs a live run, a configured GitHub App and a credential, so all eleven are **proposed**, none executed. Ten are represented in the unproven list under `## Engines`; **B4 is the exception** — on owner instruction it is recorded as an acceptance criterion the implementation phase must prove, and U2 is restated to say so rather than to leave it open.

re-verify: 2026-12-10, or earlier on any of — **the owner amending the 2026-09-12 direction this revision implements, in particular reinstating an enable variable, requiring a write-permission check in place of the `author_association` prefilter, or naming which credential shape the origin repository will use**; **`anthropics/claude-code-action` changing how it detects or prioritises the two credential shapes — `isWorkloadIdentityConfigured()` at `base-action/src/workload-identity.ts:44-48`, or the static-credential precedence at `:128-135` — either of which would change what the preflight must read**; **GitHub changing the context availability of `secrets` or `needs` in `jobs.<job_id>.if`, or the rule that a skipped job skips its dependents, either of which would invalidate the preflight mechanism outright**; **`anthropics/claude-code-action` making `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBPROCESS_ENV_SCRUB` active independently of `allowed_non_write_users`, or making a marketplace URL pinnable to a ref (`MARKETPLACE_URL_REGEX` at `base-action/src/install-plugins.ts:7-8` ceasing to anchor on `.git`), either of which would reopen a revision-4 decision**; a `v2` tag appearing on `anthropics/claude-code-action`, or the repository becoming archived; **a new published security advisory beyond `GHSA-8q5r-mmjf-575q`, *or* a new independent disclosure with no advisory, which is why re-verification must search security research and not only `repos/<o>/<r>/security-advisories`**; **`#1068` closing, or the shipped `examples/claude.yml` gaining an author-trust gate, either of which changes whether clause (d) is a local addition or upstream default**; `#1121` closing with a change to the recommended permissions; `#1482` closing, which would make the dropped `synchronize` trigger a numeric rather than a structural decision; the maintainer's own `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml` adding `synchronize`; `docs/security.md` correcting its "no `ref:` → base branch" annotation, which is wrong for `pull_request` events; `google-github-actions/run-gemini-cli` or `openai/codex-action` publishing a `v1` release series with notes; or R08, R09, R20 or R45 publishing a decision that contradicts a configuration recorded here. The version pin is stale on a scale of days at roughly one patch release per day and must be re-resolved unconditionally at implementation time.

## Parameters

- owns — **none**. R18's `## Couplings` block has an empty `owns:` line and `research/CLAUDE.md` records R18's `owns` column as `—`, so this item contributes no row to `docs/port/PARAMETERS.md`.
- assumes msrv-policy = stable minus 2 minor versions, raised only in a minor release, declared as rust-version in Cargo.toml and tested in CI
- assumes rust-edition = 2024
- assumes target-os-matrix = ubuntu-latest, macos-latest
- assumes license = MIT OR Apache-2.0
- No `CONFLICT:` line is emitted. R18's `## Couplings` block lists an empty `consumes:`, so there is no consumed parameter whose value this recommendation could need changed, and none of the four owner-fixed values above is challenged.

**How each assumed parameter bears on this decision.** The four lines above carry the registry value and nothing else, because the packaging validator reads everything after `= ` as the value and compares it byte-for-byte with `docs/port/PARAMETERS.md`; the engagement notes that revision 4 wrote on those lines are therefore stated here instead, unchanged in substance.

- `msrv-policy` is **not engaged**: neither member of the bundle adds a crate to the dependency tree, so no minimum supported Rust version is constrained by anything R18 recommends.
- `rust-edition` is **not engaged**: neither workflow file references a Rust edition or invokes `cargo`.
- `target-os-matrix` is **engaged only in the negative**: both members are deliberately single non-matrix jobs on one Linux runner and do not join the OS matrix, because the reviewed artifact is a diff rather than a build.
- `license` is **engaged and satisfied**: the single recommended component is MIT (`license.spdx_id`, `action-release.log`), and invoking a GitHub Action propagates no license term into the template's distributed artifacts.

## Empirical check

Working directory for every command: `/Users/stevemorin/c/rs-launch-blueprint-p02-plan` (except the `actionlint` invocations and the revision-3 log captures, whose working directory is `research/runs/R18/2026-09-06T043454Z-6f31d86e5b7d/review/evidence`). **Revision 6's three captures** record their own working directory in their own headers: `needs-and-credential-shapes.log` ran with its downloads staged in a session scratch directory outside the repository, and `actionlint.log`'s revision-6 addendum and `protected-regions-r6.log` ran in `research/runs/R18/2026-09-06T043454Z-6f31d86e5b7d/review/evidence`, except the one `actionlint` invocation over the scratch mutant, which names the scratch directory as its `cwd` in the log. Toolchain and OS, captured in `review/evidence/toolchain.log`:

```
$ rustc --version

$ cargo --version
cargo 1.98.0 (797e8a9bc 2026-08-05) (Homebrew)
$ uname -srm
Darwin 25.4.0 arm64          # macOS 26.4 (build 25E246)
$ actionlint --version
1.7.12
$ gh --version
gh version 2.98.0 (2026-08-20)
```

`rustc` and `cargo` are recorded because the role contract requires them; **neither member invokes them**, so they bound nothing in this decision. Every check below **verifies a claim the raw reports, the upstream issues or the audits make**; none builds the recommendation. All **nine** candidate workflow files — seven from revisions 1 to 4, plus the two added at revision 6 — are **lint fixtures under `review/evidence/`**, never under `.github/workflows/`, and each carries a header saying so. Nothing was written under `.github/workflows/` at any revision, and revision 6 wrote nothing there either. No workflow was installed, no credential was used, no provider run occurred, and nothing was written outside this run's `review/` directory.

**How to read the `Exit` column.** It names the **process exit of each command**, taken strictly from the cited log, and it is not a verdict on the check. Three commands exit **non-zero as their expected and correct result**, because for those tools a non-zero exit *is* the finding: `gh api` exits 1 on HTTP 404, and a 404 on `git/ref/tags/v2` is the evidence that no `v2` major series exists; `grep -c` exits 1 when it matches nothing, and a zero count on `ts-launch-blueprint`'s `.github/` tree is the evidence that `ts` ships no `claude` path; `actionlint` exits 1 on the negative fixture, which is the diagnostic being sought. Where a log records no exit for a command, the cell says so rather than inferring one.

**The unpinned marketplace fetch, which the auditor raised outside the three findings, is investigated and adjudicated** in `## Decision` (check 15): it is **structurally unpinnable** (`MARKETPLACE_URL_REGEX` anchors the URL end to `.git`), it clones `anthropics/claude-code@main` inside the already-credentialed job (`base-action/src/index.ts:36`, after `setupWorkloadIdentity()` at `:18`), even the marketplace *name* comes from that remote content, and the maintainer's own review reference uses no marketplace at all. **Revision 4 removes both inputs** and adopts the maintainer's pinned form. This is the one place where revision 4 changes the recommended configuration rather than the argument.

**The two auditors' shipping conclusions, quoted in full, because they differ and the difference matters.** The empirical auditor (revision 3): "No: the current evidence does not support shipping this public, write-capable workflow bundle to strangers. It supports a conditional P03 proposal after F1–F3 are corrected and the stated P03 runtime checks establish the prefilter and execution ordering. The unpinned marketplace fetch and the acknowledged trusted-author prompt-injection residual remain owner-risk reasons to choose the documented opt-in runner-up." The judgment auditor (revision 2, `review/audit-fable.r2.md`): "**Would I ship this to strangers?** Not as it stands, and not because of U2… My objection is the case it does not address: the origin repository, flag on, credential configured, public, where any account that can type a comment starts a job holding `id-token: write` and reaches the action before any trust check the workflow expresses… **That risk may well be acceptable; the recommended configuration omits the specific misconfiguration the chain needed, and I would probably still ship after seeing it argued.** But it has not been argued, and I will not certify a public-template workflow that can spend money and push commits on the strength of a security section that treats one endpoint returning one row as the security history." That auditor also wrote: "None of that reopens the recommendation to ship both workflows as one bundle, which the architecture comparison supports and which I would expect to survive."

**Where the two auditors agree, and where they do not.** Both refuse to certify revision 3 as written. Both name the same unremovable residual. They **diverge on the remedy**: the judgment auditor expects the bundle recommendation to survive once argued and explicitly declines to reopen it; the empirical auditor names the residual and the marketplace fetch as reasons to prefer the runner-up. Revision 4 does not split the difference rhetorically. It removes the marketplace fetch, which was the one item both treat as a defect rather than a judgement, and it changes the recommendation's **form** so that the part neither auditor would certify — enabling a write-capable lane on a public repository without a recorded owner acceptance — is no longer recommended.

**What revision 4 changed, and whether the recommendation moved. It moved.** Revisions 1 to 3 recommended "Ship **both** workflows … inert by default in a downstream fork", which read as an unconditional recommendation and treated origin-repository enablement as settled. Revision 4 recommends shipping both **as inert artifacts** and **withholds enablement on any public repository, the origin included, until owner acceptance of a named residual is on record**, with the runner-up promoted from ranked alternative to the default outcome if acceptance is withheld. The reasoning is set out in "What enabling costs the owner": shipping the files and enabling the capability are separate acts with separate risk owners; the residual is a property of the capability, not of the files, so the runner-up relocates the decision rather than removing the risk; but the residual is unremovable by configuration, binds **both** lanes (because S2 escalates through the App installation token, whose default request is `contents`/`pull_requests`/`issues` `write`, not through the job's grants), and sits behind a pre-token window in which the workflow's job-level `if:` is the only control — and no owner acceptance exists. On that reading a conditional recommendation is the honest one, and an unconditional "ship both" would assert an owner judgement this research does not hold. Revision 4 also does **not** claim the runner-up wins outright, because the evidence does not show it is safer — it shows it moves the decision to adoption time. The configuration changes are exactly three, all on the review lane: `plugin_marketplaces` removed, `plugins` removed, and the `prompt:` value changed from `'/code-review:code-review ${{ github.repository }}/pull/${{ github.event.pull_request.number }} --comment'` to `'/review-pr REPO: ${{ github.repository }} PR_NUMBER: ${{ github.event.pull_request.number }}'`. The assistant lane's YAML is unchanged (`diff` exit 0, check 16). Everything else in revision 4 is correction, scoping, or argument.

(iv) The `ts` agreement-level bullet keeps both exits named. (v) Check 6's observed column now records that `py`'s assistant lane has **no fork or same-repo clause**, which is the fact JA-05's fourth objection turns on.

| 2 | Each lane gains a `credential-preflight` job carrying the guards, reading both credential shapes from `jobs.<job_id>.env`, printing the skip reason through `::notice` and `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY`, and publishing a literal `credential` output that the real job gates on through `needs` | owner direction 2, implemented with the always-run preflight the owner chose on 2026-09-11 — the only shape the platform's context table permits, re-verified at checks 17 and 19 |
| 3 | Deleting the file is named as the supported opt-out, in the decision and in both fixture headers | owner direction 3 |
| 4 | The assistant lane gains **bot exclusion**, which it did not have: `github.event.sender.type != 'Bot'` plus the per-event author type check. Guard-contract clause (b) is rewritten to say it now binds both lanes, and the guard-layer table gains a row for it | owner direction 4. **This closes a gap rather than restating a control**: the r4 assistant fixture had no bot check, and `permissions.ts:114` returns `true` for every `[bot]` login, so the action does not close it downstream |
| 5 | The `author_association` prefilter is **unchanged**, and the record now says the owner considered a write-permission check and chose association deliberately | owner direction 5 |
| 6 | Both credential shapes are handled, with their detection predicate, their precedence and their per-shape setup cost read in source at `v1.0.220` | the owner has not said which shape this repository has; check 18 |
| 7 | Every statement making the recommendation conditional on an owner acceptance is **rewritten**, not annotated. The runner-up returns to being an alternative; U8 is closed; U2 is restated as acceptance criterion **B4**; the re-verify trigger that fired on "the owner recording acceptance" is replaced | the enablement condition is dissolved by the design: with no flag, installing a credential *is* the acceptance, taken by whoever installs it — **qualified at the JA-06 correction** for the repository whose credential predates the files, where no act is taken at all (row 9) |
| 8 | `B10` and `B11` are added to the acceptance criteria, for the new bot checks and for credential-shape detection | both are new controls, and neither was observed |
| 9 | **Added after the revision-6 judgment audit, which rejected revision 6 on one finding, JA-06.** A paragraph under "What installing a credential costs the owner" states the pre-existing-credential case; the three statements it falsifies (the recommendation, the section's own opening, and the "Given (1) to (4)" paragraph) each carry a reference to it, as do change-table row 7 and `## Engines` disagreement 1; the preflight description's unconditional "It prints through both channels a reader actually sees" is scoped to the `shape = none` branch; each fixture's adopter block gains one entry saying the same; **B4** and **U2** are scoped to require the pre-existing-credential path as well as the no-credential path; **U19** records the audit's non-blocking architectural observation; and two editorial defects the audit named are fixed — the minimal-example paragraph called the revision-6 fixtures revision-5 ones, and the `## Empirical check` table printed row 21 before row 20 | the audit's remedy, adopted as scoped. The owner's mechanism is not changed: no enable variable returns, no gate, permission, trigger or pin moves, and the three residual spans, the `## Supersedes` block and the four `- assumes` lines are byte-identical before and after (re-verified after every edit) |

**What revision 6 does not change, established by diff against the revision-5 blob rather than asserted** (check 20, `protected-regions-r6.log`): the pinned action version, both `on:` trigger lists, both real jobs' permission blocks, the review lane's `/review-pr` prompt, the assistant lane's absent `prompt:` and its `additional_permissions`, the four `- assumes` lines, and the residual analysis — the runner-up's third condition with its disclosure quotation, fact (3) and fact (4) — all of which are byte-for-byte identical. The unproven list stood at **eighteen rows** at revision 6, of which U2 is restated as an acceptance criterion and U8 is closed, so sixteen remained open and no row was quietly dropped. **Updated at the JA-06 correction**: U19 is added, so the list now stands at **nineteen rows** with **seventeen open**.

**How revision 6's sweep was done, because reading would have missed locations.** Before any edit, the revision-5 file was searched mechanically for the three claim subjects and the complete list of line numbers was built first: the enable variables and every form of "enable"; every statement making the recommendation conditional on owner acceptance; and every description of what an unconfigured clone does. Those three searches returned **44 lines**. A fourth search on the bare stem `enabl` found **one more** the first three had missed — "whether the capability is **enabled** at all", in the what-may-vary sentence — and that miss is recorded rather than quietly absorbed, because it is exactly the failure the enumeration discipline exists to catch. Two further locations were pulled in by the same edits rather than by the searches: `#1068`'s second exposure row, whose "no such secret under federation" answer is true only of the federation shape, and guard-contract clause (b), which named a control the assistant lane did not have. Eight more are bookkeeping the edits force — the title, the `## Dates` paragraph, the fixture count of seven, the empirical-check table, the lint bullet, the evidence inventory, the directory-change paragraph and this revision paragraph. **55 locations found, 38 edited, 17 left alone**: 13 of those 17 are revision history or verbatim quotation, where the past tense is correct, and 4 were false positives of the search patterns — two mentions of `label_trigger` being "inert", one quotation of `docs/setup.md` about federation identifiers living in repository variables, and the string "red check" inside the word "anchored check". The one place a historical quotation no longer matches the landed text is named above rather than left for an auditor to discover. After editing, the same searches were re-run against the edited file and every remaining hit was confirmed to be either a statement that a variable is removed, a historical statement in the past tense, or a false positive.

**How the JA-06 correction's sweep was done, and what it deliberately left alone.** The same discipline was applied before any edit: the current entry was searched for every line carrying the finding's subjects — `act of acceptance`, `installing a credential`, `whoever installs`, `will see it`, `announce`, `the switch`, `moment they install`, `refus`, `notice`, `step summary`, `annotation` — and the complete list was built first. **Seven locations carry the claim**: the recommendation in `## Decision`; the opening of "What installing a credential costs the owner"; fact (2) of that section; the "Given (1) to (4)" paragraph; change-table row 7; `## Engines` disagreement 1; and the preflight description in `## Decision`, whose sentence "It prints through both channels a reader actually sees" asserted the printing unconditionally and is now **scoped to the `shape = none` branch**, with the other branch's one line of ordinary log text named. **Six were edited. One, fact (2), was deliberately left byte-identical** — it sits among the residual facts the owner placed out of scope, and appending to it would change text immediately before the anchored span the audit re-verified — so the qualification names fact (2) explicitly instead, which is the direction of reference this discipline permits provided it is disclosed rather than silent. This is that disclosure. The two remaining statements the searches returned about the `::notice` and the step summary — fact (1), and change-table row 2's "printing the skip reason" — name the skip on their face, so both are true as they stand and neither was edited. Bookkeeping the edits force, each verified against the file rather than assumed: the unproven-row count, the change table, the `- assumes` line numbers in check 20, the directory-change paragraph, the reproducibility disclosure, this paragraph, and **the one fixture line citation this correction introduced** — the adopter-block entry adds six lines to each fixture header, so every line number below it moves by six, and the citation above is the post-edit one, checked with `sed -n '145p;160p'` on both files. A `grep -o 'r5\.yml:[0-9-]*'` over this entry returns that citation and no other, so no pre-existing fixture line reference went stale.

**Engines.**

- codex `raw/codex.md` (gpt-5.6-terra, retrieved 2026-09-06) — recommends **neither** workflow plus a documented opt-in recipe at `v1.0.217`. Raised **one** `BASELINE-REVIEW:` line.
- opus `raw/opus.md` (claude-opus-5[1m], retrieved 2026-09-09) — recommends **both**, bundled, at `v1.0.220`, inert behind a repository variable. Raised **two**.
- doxa-openai `raw/doxa-openai.normalized.md` (gpt-5.6-sol, retrieved 2026-09-09) — recommends **neither** active workflow; treats F051 and F052 as independent decisions; documents an optional recipe at `v1.0.219` pinned to a commit SHA. Raised **two**, and is the source of all four JA-05 objections plus the trusted-association prefilter this decision adopts.
- doxa-gemini `raw/doxa-gemini.normalized.md` (deep-research-preview-04-2026, retrieved 2026-09-09) — recommends **both**, bundled, pinned at `v1.0.175`, with a named bot **denylist** it calls Rust-specific. Raised **one** (inside backticks). It is also the only engine that surfaced the S2 disclosure.
- doxa-perplexity `raw/doxa-perplexity.normalized.md` (sonar-deep-research, retrieved 2026-09-09) — recommends **both**, present but "intentionally inert", pinned at floating `@v1`, guarded by an `if` that "checks for the presence of the required secret". Raised **one**.
- The Doxa regroup `raw/doxa.normalized.md` merges the three Doxa bodies per template field; `doxa/normalization.json` records the repairs.

**Weight given to `doxa-perplexity`.** Its body is missing five top-level fields (`Compatibility`, `Parameters`, `Migration implications`, `Validation strategy`, `Confidence & re-verify trigger`) and its `@mention` member was demoted for carrying only two of the fifteen required H5 fields. It is therefore weighed for what it contains — a trigger list, a permission set, and the inert-in-forks intent — and **carries no weight at all on parameters, compatibility or validation**. Where its evidence is missing, the conclusions below rest on the other four engines and on the checks above.

**Weight given to `doxa-gemini`, revised at revision 3.** Revision 2 discounted gemini's security material after refuting its version figure. That refutation was wrong (fitness gate 3), and gemini turns out to be the only engine that surfaced the most consequential item in this record. Its **security sourcing is therefore credited**; its **pin** and its **bot denylist** remain rejected on the independent grounds in §§2–3 below. The correction is about how a mistaken collateral detail was allowed to discard a correct substantive lead, which is a synthesis failure rather than an engine failure.

**Disagreements, and what settled each.**

1. **Ship both, or neither?** Split 3–2 (opus, gemini, perplexity vs codex, doxa-openai). The "neither" case rests on four grounds, which divide two and two. **Two are engineering claims that the recommended design answers syntactically but not yet at runtime**: checks 1, 7, 12, 17 and 19 establish that a job-level `if:` may read `github`, `needs`, `vars` and `inputs` and may not read `secrets`, that `secrets` *is* readable in a job's `env`, and that the preflight relay between the two lints clean and type-checks — so a public template *can* ship a workflow that stays silent until a credential it does not itself supply is installed, and that is prefiltered on the commenter's association, imposing no credential on a consumer. **Revised at revision 6**: revisions **2** to 5 phrased this as a workflow "gated on a variable it does not itself set" — revision 1 did not use that wording, and the span is read from each revision's bytes rather than assumed (check 21) — which the owner's direction removes; the gate is credential presence. Whether an unconfigured clone yields a `skipped` job rather than a red check is still not observed — the platform rule is documented (check 17) and the run is not — and on owner instruction it is now **acceptance criterion B4** rather than the open unknown **U2**. **The other two grounds** — that a public template should not carry a vendor-AI exemplar, and that no acceptance criterion requires AI review — are **owner policy**, and are carried as the ranked runner-up. Revision 3 adds a **third** owner-policy ground to the runner-up, and it is the one the judgment audit asked to see argued: the residual prompt-injection exposure of a write-capable assistant lane on a public repository, reachable from trusted-author text that may quote untrusted content. Chosen at revisions 1 to 3: **both**, unconditionally. Revised at revision 4 and carried by revision 5: both files, as inert artifacts, with enablement on any public repository gated on a recorded owner acceptance, and the "neither" position promoted to the default if acceptance was withheld. **Revised again at revision 6, on the owner's 2026-09-12 direction: both files, shipped with the template by default, each lane gated on credential presence, with deletion as the opt-out — and the conditional form dissolved, because the design leaves no flag for the owner to set and installing a credential is itself the act of acceptance.** **Qualified at the JA-06 correction**: for a downstream repository that already holds a credential for another purpose no act is taken, nothing is printed, and both lanes go live by default — stated under "What installing a credential costs the owner", and the reason revision 6 exposes more than revision 5 downstream. What supports shipping the files is unchanged and is stated rather than assumed — the phase-by-phase analysis in fitness gate 3 finds the recommended configuration outside every phase of the published chain, **three** of the disclosure's four remediations are active in this configuration (the scrub is not — finding F2), and clause (d) closes the one exposure that credential presence structurally cannot reach. The *third* ground, the owner-policy residual, is **not** withdrawn and is **not** declared acceptable here: it returns to being what revision 3 made it, a condition under which an owner would choose the runner-up, and the owner exercises it per repository by not installing a credential, or by deleting the file. If B4 or U12 fails in P03, the "neither" case wins outright and this decision is revised against this recorded research.
2. **Is the bot guard Rust-specific?** `doxa-gemini` asserts a Rust-specific adjustment and proposes a name denylist containing `dependabot[bot]`, `renovate[bot]` and `release-plz[bot]`. **Settled against it** by check 6: `py`'s guard is *type*-based (`github.event.pull_request.user.type != 'Bot'` and `github.event.sender.type != 'Bot'`), which is name-independent and therefore already catches `release-plz[bot]` and every other bot without enumeration; a denylist is strictly weaker. Check 10 adds a second, independent reason a denylist is the wrong instrument: the action's own `checkWritePermissions` returns `true` for **any** actor whose login ends in `[bot]`, and the refusal lives in `checkHumanActor`, which is likewise type-based — so a name list at the workflow level would be duplicating, in a weaker form, a check that already exists in a stronger form one layer down. The HIGH question in the prompt answers: **`py`'s guard logic ports unchanged; there is no Rust-specific adjustment to make.**
3. **Which version?** `v1.0.217` (codex), `v1.0.219` (doxa-openai), `v1.0.175` (gemini), `@v1` floating (perplexity), `v1.0.220` (opus). Checks 2 and 3 settle it: all three specific tags resolve, each was current at its own retrieval date, `v1.0.220` is current at 2026-09-10, and cadence is ≈ one patch per day — so **no engine was wrong, and every pin is stale within days**. `doxa-openai`'s SHA `5ccc3a35a6367cdb8e6fbd0728287467540ecfe2` is confirmed as the exact commit behind `v1.0.219`. **`doxa-gemini`'s `v1.0.175` is rejected on currency, not on safety, and revision 3 corrects revision 2 here**: gemini's safety argument for it is sound — `v1.0.175` is above the `v1.0.94` at which the S2 chain was fixed and above the `1.0.74` that fixes S1 — and revision 2's refutation of its `v1.0.94` figure compared that figure against a different advisory and a single release's changelog, which was a category error (fitness gate 3). The remaining ground is that `v1.0.175` is `py`'s pin, 45 patch releases behind current, on a component releasing daily. `doxa-perplexity`'s `@v1` is the *form* of a pin, which is **R20**'s decision — but note S1's own guidance that non-pinned references received the fix automatically, and note that `plugin_marketplaces` takes a Git URL that `MARKETPLACE_URL_REGEX` requires to end in `.git`, so it cannot carry a ref at all (check 15). Both are handed to R20 as evidence; the second is also **acted on here**, by removing the input rather than pinning it, because it admits no pin.
4. **How should a lane stay silent in a repository that has not configured it?** `doxa-perplexity` proposes an `if` that checks for the presence of the required secret. **Its form was refuted and its substance is now adopted, and revision 6 records both halves rather than only the first.** The form stays refuted by checks 1, 7 and 17: the context table omits `secrets` from `jobs.<job_id>.if`, and `actionlint` rejects `if: secrets.X != ''` with exit 1 and the quoted diagnostic — re-run at revision 6, not cited. A one-line secret-presence job condition is not merely unproven, it is not expressible. **But the requirement behind it — gate on the credential, not on a separate flag — is what the owner directed on 2026-09-12, and revision 6 implements it** through the only route the context table leaves open: read the secret in `jobs.<job_id>.env` in a preflight job, publish a literal output, and let the real job read `needs`. **Revisions 1 to 5** adopted `opus`'s `vars`-gated flag instead — every revision, not only the later ones, which check 21 establishes by reading each revision's own bytes; that flag is **removed at revision 6**. `doxa-perplexity` was weighed for very little in this record (see "Weight given to `doxa-perplexity`"), and on this point its instinct was right while its expression was illegal; saying so is the honest reading. The step-level `env`-relay alternative it suggests in the same breath remains legal and remains rejected, because a skipped *step* hides inside a green job.
5. **Drop `synchronize` from the review trigger?** Only `opus` proposes it; `py`, `gemini` and `perplexity` keep it; `doxa-openai` keeps it too but pairs it with per-PR `concurrency`. It is **adopted, on a structural argument, not a measured one**: `synchronize` is the only trigger that fires per push rather than per state change, and the maintainer's own review workflow listens on `types: [opened]` alone (check 4). No cost figure exists to make this numeric (`#1482`, open). It remains a weak element of this recommendation and is **U6**.
6. **Bundle or two independent decisions?** `doxa-openai` argues independence; `opus` supplies the only actual *argument* either way, and it is a design coupling: dropping `synchronize` removes automatic re-review after a fix-up push, and the replacement is a write-access user typing `@claude please re-review`, which is F052. **That argument holds only if `synchronize` is dropped** — if P03 restores it, the bundling rationale weakens to shared component, credential, App installation, pin and Dependabot surface, which is real but not decisive. `doxa-openai`'s further ask, to split F052's **ledger** decision from F051's, is a controller matter and is routed as a baseline finding rather than answered here.
7. **`issues: [assigned]` in the assistant trigger.** `codex` would drop it ("a template needs an explicit request, not a side effect of triage"); `opus`, `py`, `examples/claude.yml` and the maintainer's own file all keep it. **Kept**, on three-way reference concordance (check 4), and recorded as minor: with `assignee_trigger` unset (`action.yml` at `v1.0.220` gives it no default), the expected consequence is that an `assigned` event only re-evaluates an issue whose body or title already contains `@claude`. That is **reasoned from the input's absent default and the workflow's own `contains(…, '@claude')` condition, not observed**.
8. **Small factual corrections to the raws**, recorded so they are not carried forward: `ts` has **8** workflow files at `cb1cbcb`, not nine (check 6); `releases/latest` returns the floating tag `v1` (published 2025-08-26), so the newest *patch* must be read from the releases list; the maintainer's own workflows use `actions/checkout@v6` while `py` uses `@v7`, and `actions/checkout`'s current release is `v7.0.1` (2026-07-20), so `py` is the more current of the two on that line.
9. **Not surfaced by any engine, found here:** the one published GitHub security advisory for the action, `GHSA-8q5r-mmjf-575q` / `CVE-2026-47751` (check 5). It does not change the recommendation — `v1.0.220` and `py`'s `v1.0.175` are both above the patched `1.0.74` — but it is direct evidence for the fork guard, for `docs/security.md`'s "do not check out an untrusted ref into the workspace root before this action", and for R20's pin-form tradeoff. Revision 3 records that it is **not the whole security history** (fitness gate 3).

**Four trigger and guard objections from `doxa-openai`, adjudicated at revision 3 (JA-05).** All four were raised in `raw/doxa-openai.normalized.md`; revision 2 answered none of them.

10. **Draft double-fire — `opened` plus `ready_for_review` with no `draft` clause. ADOPTED.** `doxa-openai` recommends `draft == false` (`:168-175`). The objection is correct and it contradicts this decision's own bounded-spend rationale: `opus`'s stated ground for the trigger list, "every retained event fires at most once per pull request per state change" (`raw/opus.md:105`), is true of each event and **false of their composition**, because a pull request opened as a draft fires `opened` and then fires `ready_for_review` when it is marked ready — two paid agent sessions for one pull request. With `draft == false`, a draft-opened pull request is skipped at `opened` and reviewed once at `ready_for_review`, and a ready-opened pull request is reviewed once at `opened` and never fires `ready_for_review`; the composition becomes exactly-once. The clause is adopted, lints clean (check 12) and is fixture `candidate-claude-code-review.r3.yml`. **Its basis is reasoned and practice-corroborated, not observed**: the documented `pull_request` activity-type set includes `opened`, `ready_for_review` and `converted_to_draft` (check 11), and `github/docs`' own repository carries six workflow files gating on `pull_request.draft`; no run was executed, so exactly-once firing is **U11**. `doxa-openai`'s companion proposal, per-PR `concurrency` with `cancel-in-progress: true` (`:236-239`), is **declined with reasons**: it exists to cancel superseded runs from a per-push trigger, and with `synchronize` dropped and `draft == false` added, no retained event can fire twice for one pull-request state, so there is nothing for it to cancel. If P03 restores `synchronize` (U6), the concurrency group must be restored with it.
11. **`pull_request_review: [submitted]`. KEPT, with the widening stated.** `doxa-openai` excludes it (`:348`) because it "adds a fourth content surface not requested and makes quoted mentions in review summaries capable of triggering work". Two facts bear on it. First, the objection is right that this **widens the prompt's MEDIUM question**, which names three surfaces — issue comments, PR review comments, issue bodies — and the recommendation ships four plus title matching; revision 2 shipped the widening without noting it, and revision 3 notes it here. Second, `py`, `examples/claude.yml` and the maintainer's own `claude.yml` are character-identical on this event (check 4), and clause (d) now bounds *who* can write into that surface to `OWNER`/`MEMBER`/`COLLABORATOR`, which is the property that makes surface count matter far less than it did before. Kept on that basis. The narrower three-surface list remains a defensible configuration and is the first thing to cut if P03 finds the assistant lane noisy.
12. **Issue-title matching. KEPT.** `doxa-openai` excludes it (`:350`), "only issue bodies are in scope". The clause `contains(github.event.issue.title, '@claude')` is `py`'s, `examples/claude.yml`'s and the maintainer's own, character-identical (check 4). An issue title is authored by the issue author and therefore falls under the same clause-(d) prefilter as the body, so it adds no new author surface — only a second field on the same event. Kept, and recorded as the least load-bearing element of the trigger configuration.
13. **No fork or same-repository clause on the assistant job. THE CONTRACT WAS WRONG, NOT THE FIXTURE.** `doxa-openai` asks for a same-repository preflight on this lane (`:388`, `:416`, `:479`); guard-contract clause (a) at revision 2 read "the job never runs on a pull request from a fork" as a property of the whole contract, while only `candidate-claude-code-review.yml` implements it — and `py`'s own `claude.yml` has no such clause either (check 6). The resolution is that **clause (a) binds the review lane only**, and revision 3 rewrites it that way with the reason: none of the assistant lane's four events is a `pull_request` event, so `GITHUB_REF` is the default branch, no fork ref is checked out, and running in the base repository with base-repository credentials is the design rather than a leak. What `doxa-openai`'s preflight would actually address — the agent *operating on* a fork pull request's contents — is covered by `docs/security.md`'s "do not check out an untrusted ref into the workspace root", which the assistant fixture already satisfies by passing no `ref:` on non-`pull_request` events. The assistant lane's real fork-adjacent exposure is a **comment** on a fork pull request from an untrusted account, which is `#1068`, answered by clause (d).

**Two record gaps closed at revision 3, and one of them reopened and settled at revision 4.** Revision 2 omitted `plugin_marketplaces: 'https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code.git'` from the `## Decision` table's action inputs even though `candidate-claude-code-review.yml` carried it and the `plugins` reference cannot resolve without it; revision 3 listed it, and recorded `opus`'s observation that the review lane fetches its plugin from an external marketplace Git URL at run time — "one more moving part and one more trust edge" (`raw/opus.md:589`) — with the fact that a marketplace URL carries no ref and cannot be pinned, then **handed it to R20 instead of deciding it**. That hand-off was the defect the empirical audit found. **Revision 4 decides it**: the two inputs are removed from the recommended configuration (see `## Decision`, "The unpinned marketplace fetch, adjudicated", and check 15), and only the general pin-form question — that some run-time references admit no ref at all — remains R20's. `opus`'s observation was right; recording it as evidence for another item, while continuing to recommend the configuration it indicts, was not.

**Every remaining security-relevant claim, checked against its source.** This table exists because the judgment audit's objection was that a security signal was fetched, reduced to a datum, and never adjudicated. Each row names the claim, where it now rests, and its status.

| # | Security-relevant claim | Source, and what was actually checked | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | "The action can only be triggered by users with write access" | `docs/security.md:5` **and** `src/github/validation/permissions.ts` at `v1.0.220`, read in source (check 10) | **Standing, with a qualification revision 3 adds**: `:114` returns `true` for any actor whose login ends in `[bot]`, so the prose is true of *user* actors only; App actors are refused by `checkHumanActor`, a separate function |
| 2 | "By default, GitHub Apps and bots cannot trigger this action" | `docs/security.md:6`, `action.yml` `allowed_bots: ""`, **and** `src/github/validation/actor.ts:70` plus both mode entry points (check 10) | **Standing and now read in source.** It is an S2 remediation, not an original design property |
| 3 | `allowed_non_write_users` left at `""`, so the three `runner.os` guards are unreached | `action.yml` at `v1.0.220` (revision-2 addendum) | **Standing.** Revision 3 adds that this same default is what places the recommended configuration outside the S2 chain's Phase 1 |
| 4 | `GHSA-8q5r-mmjf-575q` is patched in `1.0.74`, below both `v1.0.220` and `py`'s `v1.0.175` | `…/security-advisories` (check 5) | **Standing** |
| 5 | "Exactly one published advisory" is the security history | `…/security-advisories` returning `length: 1` | **Refuted at revision 3.** S2 exists, is rated 7.8, had variants exploited in the wild, and has **no GitHub advisory** (check 9). The endpoint is a partial view by construction |
| 6 | The fork guard produces a skip rather than a red check | maintainer workflow comment and `py`'s own comment about PR #470 (`source-repos-pinned.log`) | **Unproven — U3.** Reference concordance plus one field report, no observed run |
| 7 | Federation removes the stored credential | `docs/setup.md` at `v1.0.220`, both maintainer workflows (check 4) | **Standing, and narrowed at revision 3**: it does not remove the OIDC exposure, which is `#1068`'s third row and S2's exfiltration target |
| 8 | `docs/security.md`'s prompt-injection sanitization protects the assistant lane | `docs/security.md:78`, `:16`, `:181` (check 10) **and `action.yml:228`, `:301` (check 14)** | **Re-weighted at revision 3 and narrowed further at revision 4.** The document says "new bypass techniques may emerge"; several mitigations are S2 remediations; the S2 researcher reports ~50 permission-system bypasses. **Revision 4 removes one mitigation from the standing set entirely**: the subprocess environment scrub is *available at the tag but inactive in this configuration*, because `action.yml:301` emits it only when `allowed_non_write_users != ''`, which this decision leaves `""`. Where it does fire the document still calls it "best-effort" and says it "reduces but does not eliminate" |
| 9 | `isBodySafeToUse` closes the S2 Phase-2 edit vector | `src/github/data/fetcher.ts:312`, `:530` (check 10) | **Standing as a definition read at the tag, with two limits**: it falls open when no trigger time is available (`if (!triggerTime) return true`), and **its call site in the issue-body path was not traced**, so "closes the vector" is read from the function and its comments, not from the path — U14 |

Only the general pin-form question goes to R20 |

It is therefore **not readable from the action's source at any tag**, and the `contents: read` on the job says nothing about it. This matters because it is the token S2 escalates through | in P03, run the review lane once and call `GET /installation/repositories` or read the token's own permission set from the run log, then compare against the `contents`/`pull_requests`/`issues` `write` default that `token.ts:69-73` applies when `additional_permissions` is set; hand the result to **R08** |
| **U18** | **New at revision 4, and it is the cost of the marketplace removal.** That a template-local `.claude/commands/review-pr.md` reproduces what the removed `code-review@claude-code-plugins` plugin did — that it resolves as a slash command inside the action's Claude session, that it can post, and that the review it produces is comparable in quality. Nothing here proves any of the three; the fixture proves only that the YAML is legal (check 16). Revision 5 carried those clauses on the privileged job's own condition. A later hand that "simplifies" the gate, or adds `always()` or another continuation override, would silently remove the actor gate while leaving a workflow that still looks guarded. **The evidence that this line is easy to get wrong is revision 6's own fixtures**, which shipped the negative predicate `!= 'none'` first and had to be corrected in a second capture (`actionlint.log`, revision-6 second addendum).
